The Unpaid Understudy","volume":"婚后外传：海边剧场的暴雨夜","anchor":"chapter-0126","paragraphs":["The morning after the storm, Mara woke with a paper crown beside her pillow and Cassian's arm warm around her waist. The puppet astronomer had acquired a second note: PAYMENT FOR SERVICES TO THE PLAYHOUSE. \"A satisfactory fee,\" she said. \"Although your rescue performance was a little under-rehearsed.\"","\"I was distracted by my leading lady.\"","She turned in his arms.","Downstairs in the hotel, the emergency desk had become a recovery hub. The internet was back, but the noticeboard remained useful for people without phones. The motel owner brought clean towels. Iris planned an informal performance, using the puppet and whatever scenery had survived. The parachute cloth was folded and returned. The craft hut was dry, and a volunteer could knit replacements for the soaked costume cuffs. The old loom had escaped damage because people had moved it early, rather than argue over who owned the floor beneath it.","Dane's absence was short-lived. He returned with a solicitor and insisted the pavilion was his masterpiece. The council officer said he could still submit a plan; he could not manufacture its necessity. His earlier non-committal replies had frustrated the staff. The council would take a tough line on access to safety equipment. A controversial design deserved debate, but the pump removal required a separate investigation. Mara asked him to restate his explanation beside the dated instruction. His answers were recorded without a theatrical accusation of guilt.","Volunteers refused to lose control of the recovery budget through another sham emergency. The newsstand owner said she would not overpay for a room in a glass box while the theatre stood repairable. Her political view differed from Iris's on plenty of questions. Today they agreed that a shared pump was not a bargaining chip. The officer promised to uphold inspections, including the stadium arrangement. The staff could use the grievance procedure without a manager obstructing them. Nobody would be made to part with a home or workshop merely because a glossy drawing looked expensive.","Mara visited the fisherman before his hospital transfer. The paramedic checked his comfort and reviewed the earlier morphine record with the receiving team. A volunteer replenished the used dressings and counted the unopened lancet packets. Care had a quiet aftermath too. The granddaughter showed Mara a snapshot of her grandfather smiling beside his rescued boat. She had stopped asking whether he would be punished for getting stranded.","At the overpass, workers had recovered the passenger car. The road to the runway was opening, though no plane would depart until the crew completed its checks. The coastguard returned the borrowed paddle. Cassian carried it to the rack while Mara used a rag to wipe mud from the handle. His wet sneaker had left a black print on his borrowed pants. She promised to keep the photograph private if he bought breakfast.","The observatory engineer held a rehearsal for the children's programme. A model satellite revolved above the stage while a magnet moved a hidden pointer. Pip explained the limits of radar, then let a younger child try the sonar demonstration in a tank. The model spaceship had lost a fin, so Iris would stitch a fabric replacement until a new one could be made. A rocket made of folded paper carried a message from the audience to the puppet. The old missile exhibit stayed outside; its history did not make it a toy.","Near the repaired shed, the electrician labelled the pump plug clearly. The checked circuit could carry its intended load; overload would again leave people vulnerable. A transparent shield protected the switch from accidental knocks. The cardboard reactor was dry enough to return to the exhibition. Pip wanted to remake its label, adding that it was a model. No visitor should have to guess what was dangerous and what was merely damp.","For the evening performance, the pottery moon hung above a megalith made of painted cardboard. A ceremonial spear became the pointer for an absent-minded professor. The actor who wanted to ski down a painted mountain was firmly limited to sliding on a mat. Another tried to skate across the stage and landed in a heap of lace. Cassian caught the falling curtain before its rod struck anyone. \"An unpaid understudy,\" Iris announced. He bowed to thunderous applause.","Mara worked backstage with a small implement for loosening knots. She repaired a slit in the backdrop with one strong stitch, then found a missing stocking behind the scenery. Iris had no wish to turn every relic of the storm into a monument. She wanted useful things to become useful again. A worn saddle went back to its owner, not into an exhibition about heroic volunteers. The ancestral sewing box stayed on her worktable.","The jobcentre reopened its workshop between performances. The adviser could accommodate people who worked shifts. Pip practised a test-taking skill by dividing one difficult question into steps. The stimulating session helped a high achiever and a struggling reader alike. A compulsory timed course had previously made him panic; this quieter session helped. An editor-in-chief from the local paper interviewed him about practical training, then agreed to let him edit his own quoted words. \"I don't want to be a miracle,\" he said. \"I want to learn.\"","The programme celebrated modernism without pretending old buildings had no value. Its designer used a stencil to print the title on reclaimed card. The photocopier still worked, to nobody’s surprise. A single staple held the pages. The proceeds would help pave the approach and restore the roof. Volunteers chose to stock up on repair supplies gradually, checking what they already had. They would attach importance to regular checks and rein in waste. They would not get anywhere by spending the same money twice.","At the final bow, Iris vigorously thanked every helper. She asked them to put emphasis on the ordinary work after the applause. Rebuilding trust took longer than drying a costume, though shared effort could stimulate it. Iris reluctantly left the stage to mingle with her audience. The council would build up a maintenance reserve and publish how it was spent. That was less dazzling than Dane's ultra-modern pavilion and more likely to endure. The need for care was timeless.","Later, Mara and Cassian walked to the beach to quench their thirst with cold lemonade. A short stock market bulletin came from the closed café. They walked toward the waves. She put her empty cup in her purse and took his hand. Behind them, the stage lights glowed through the repaired window, and the little astronomer was finally allowed to sleep."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1092,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0126\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：海边剧场的暴雨夜</div><h1>3. data-go=\"124\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"126\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x011c01","title":"1. A Very Small Holiday Favour","volume":"婚后外传：港口诊所与失踪记录","anchor":"chapter-0127","paragraphs":["Cassian booked a vacation home with a sea view. The sea promptly hid behind fog, leaving Mara a view of his bare shoulder and a kettle that whistled like an angry bird. She considered the exchange favourable. Not long ago, a private morning had seemed an impossible luxury. Now he was trying to cook eggs without setting off an alarm, while she sat on the kitchen counter judging him with affectionate severity.","Noor interrupted by telephone. A non-profit clinic near the port needed a restorer to open a jammed document drawer, not a duchess to smile at a camera. Mara promised to attend to it after breakfast. Cassian tried to quit his battle with the pan. She fed him a piece of toast and appointed him driver.","The clinic occupied a former caravanserai beside a converted tobacco storehouse. Its upper storey contained a small transport museum, and the lower rooms served travellers and dock workers. The route from the subway crossed a square where a retired submarine stood as a memorial. Beyond it, a tractor hauled produce from a nearby farm to the supermarket. A sign explained the current transit changes. Transportation was ordinary here, until one missed connection made a working day collapse.","Inside, an amplifier carried a loud announcement from the courtyard booth. A children's balloon struck Cassian's hat brim. He caught its string and returned it to a boy beside a baby's cradle. At the buffet, the cook was checking an additive on a label for a visitor with a dietary question. The mother thanked him without recognising him, which he clearly enjoyed. Mara fastened the clasp of her dolman. Cassian adjusted his belt, then they followed Noor past the cafeteria to the cramped cold room.","A vaccine shipment had arrived with incomplete temperature records. The thermostat display looked normal, but that did not establish what had happened in transit. Noor had set the shipment aside for professional review. She would not use uncertain stock. The restrictive delivery contract named an unfamiliar subcontractor, though the clinic had paid for a fully recorded journey. A driver called Mina waited with her wallet clenched in both hands.","\"They told me a delay could trigger a loss of pay,\" she said. \"The shipping app wouldn't accept my objection.\"","The jammed drawer held the paper receipt. Mara used a small accessory from her tool roll to ease the bent runner. Metal filings showed recent damage. This had not happened through ordinary wear. A compact hand-held logger sat underneath the forms, its dial scratched and its recording disc missing. The drawer was supposed to bolster the clinic's evidence; instead, somebody had tried to compress its contents out of sight.","Mina's automobile had broken down the previous evening. A man from the depot had substituted another vehicle and told her to sign after arrival. She had refused. Now the dispatcher called her overprotective and threatened to dismiss her. This was the tipping point after months of warnings. The infuriating part, she said, was that she had followed the rule everyone claimed to value. Noor asked her to sit. A chair and a calm conversation could counterbalance the strain of a high stress condition, even if they could not erase it.","Upstairs, schoolchildren crowded around a telegraph demonstration. A turbine model rotated behind glass; its flywheel turned slowly under a protective cover. A retired aircraft mechanic explained how an airline checked a delivery record. Mara heard Mina say she had wanted to train for that kind of work. The clinic's scholarship would have helped, but the depot supervisor said she must quit complaining first. Politically, she had no friends who could call an official for her.","\"You have colleagues,\" Noor said. \"Let's begin there.\"","They passed a historical medicine display on the way to the office. A bottle labelled cocaine sat behind glass, among objects no longer used by the clinic. In a separate cupboard, a patient's prescription list included Celebrex; Noor took the question about it privately. The old bottles were not instructions for present treatment. A scholarly caption gave sources, while a conversational guide explained why the collection should educate visitors rather than invite experimentation.","Outside, wind made a loose banner whip violently against a post. Mina pointed to a garage beyond the ditch. The substitute vehicle had come from there. A ferry was loading behind it, beside an export warehouse selling ceramic ware. An anonymous message now warned Mina that her job would vanish if she returned. She showed it to Noor instead of obeying it. The clinic copied the message and contacted the appropriate investigator.","The clinic director blamed short-termism in the contract. A cheap headline price had encouraged burdensome hidden charges and poor supervision. His own stock options in the transport firm created a conflict he had not declared. He said the omission was accidental. Noor's unaffected expression gave him no help. Neither fame nor a friendly manner could make that egregious omission disappear.","Mara went back for her sunscreen before the fog lifted. Cassian offered to carry her bag, then laughed when she accused him of being overprotective too. \"Only of the sandwiches,\" he said. \"They are excellent.\"","He was less amused by the manager who tried to escort her out by the elbow. She moved aside before the hand closed. Cassian did not need to raise his voice. The manager stepped back.","They would traverse the dock on the marked public route, not break into a garage. Mara placed the textual delivery instructions beside a port map and marked the timings strategically. A visit to the zoo had been planned for the afternoon; a tourist brochure still protruded from her bag. It could wait. In the small toilet she washed the dust from her hands, then joined Mina at the doorway. A bronze tomb marker from a demolished quay chapel stood nearby. Its inscription promised remembrance. The Passenger at the Ferry","volume":"婚后外传：港口诊所与失踪记录","anchor":"chapter-0128","paragraphs":["The garage owner met them with a broom and an offended expression. He had not hired Mina, sold a vehicle or promised a guaranteed cold delivery. He repaired engines. His paperwork showed that the depot had borrowed a van and returned it with a damaged brake. He had photographs. The man who collected it had signed a false name, but he had also smiled directly at the security camera.","Mara studied the image beneath an airline poster. The visitor wore a scarf as adornment and an elastic cuff on his jacket. Mina recognised the jacket: the depot supervisor had worn it while calling her unreliable. His assistant had described the substitute van as unaffected by the change. It was an infuriating reversal. Not long ago, he had accused Mina of being overprotective about the same records. The photograph suggested that the assistant had helped arrange it. Politically convenient reassurance had replaced a checkable record.","\"I don't want to quit,\" Mina said. \"I want the job I was promised.\"","Cassian leaned against the automobile outside. \"That is a reasonably modest ambition.\"","At the ferry gateway, the supervisor tried to vanish among crates of ceramic ware. He had purchased a commuter ticket, though his office lay inland. Mara applied sunscreen while she waited. They had to traverse the public quay from the subway exit; nothing in the transit map justified entering restricted areas. The investigator had asked them to stay within sight and not confront him alone. Mara therefore bought two coffees and chose the bench opposite his. Her dolman concealed a ridiculous tourist shirt. Cassian had bought it for her at the zoo stall the day before; she intended to repay him with an even worse one.","Wind shook the terminal violently. A banner began to whip over the edge of the roof. The supervisor reached to grab a loose folder before it blew away. Cassian caught the papers against his own chest and offered them back one page at a time. Each page went past Mina, who recognised her forged signature. The supervisor left the rest on the bench and ran straight toward the arriving investigator.","The supervisor had caught his belt on a crate in his haste. The folder held the missing recording disc. A textual note ordered a logger change before the delivery; an attached receipt placed the spare unit at the depot. This did not prove the vaccine safe or unsafe. It did show that the claimed continuous record had been interrupted. Noor would keep the affected supplies separate and seek an authorised replacement. No patient would become an experiment to protect a transport invoice.","They returned through the square beside the submarine. A tractor slowed near the supermarket while a porter shifted a barrel out of its path. The clinic's transportation problem was partially solved, but a new shipment still had to arrive. The ferry staff offered storage space for ordinary supplies, and the farm donated vegetables. An official medical courier would handle the replacement vaccine. Different goods needed different safeguards, however inconvenient that was to the schedule.","Inside the old caravanserai, the director faced a staff meeting. He gave a conceited speech about his flagship project. Noor asked again about his stock options. He said the details were time-consuming. Mina said the same could be said of losing a wage. The board agreed to inspect the egregious omission and the procurement contract. His influence could not put right a record merely by making the room uncomfortable.","Mara opened the compact logger case for the investigator without touching its electronic parts. More filings fell from the damaged lock. She bagged neither machine nor trace herself; the investigator arranged their preservation. The supervisor had tried to compress too many loose pages into the drawer and bent its runner. A minor attempt at concealment had created the visible damage that brought Mara there. Not every important clue was elegant.","Upstairs, the telegraph guide kept the children occupied with messages about the weather. The aircraft mechanic had volunteered to repair the display turbine, though he would not run it with an exposed flywheel. His retirement income did not stretch to free parts. Cassian paid the agreed cost without demanding a plaque. Mara kissed his cheek behind the staircase. \"The whole storehouse might survive without your name on it.\"","\"An appalling oversight.\"","The second storey had a cosy reading corner. Noor found Mina there with a cup from the buffet, still too anxious to eat. A loud laugh from the corridor made her flinch. Noor changed the evening plan accordingly. The threat had not vanished from her body just because the supervisor was being questioned. Noor spoke in her usual conversational voice. She would attend to the employment referral tomorrow; tonight Mina could call her sister and decide where she wanted to sleep.","A baby slept in a cradle near the reception booth. The sister arrived carrying a blanket she had made to upholster a child's reading seat. She would use it as a bolster for now. The clinic had a small bedroom for staff caught between shifts, and its cellar held spare bedding. A cold compress helped Mina's headache while Noor assessed what else she needed. A sign reminded visitors not to dial emergency numbers for booking questions. Cassian took the ordinary reception telephone, sparing the nurse one more interruption.","Near the historical cocaine bottle, a visitor asked about Celebrex and an old prescription. Noor moved that discussion into the consulting room. Neither a museum label nor a friendly volunteer could replace individual care. The scholarly caption remained untouched. The director's restrictive rules about who could use a staff chair, on the other hand, were suspended. No written policy justified leaving an exhausted driver standing.","At sunset the thermostat was checked by a qualified technician. The tobacco storehouse had become much more than an interesting shell; it needed reliable maintenance. Its furnace was old, and the disposal route behind the toilet required clearing. The board would not take the staff's unpaid labour for granted. Short-termism had become a costly habit. Strategically, they needed fewer heroic afternoons and more ordinary resources.","Mara found Cassian in the courtyard beside the tomb marker. He had placed the forgotten zoo brochure in her wallet. Their vacation home waited across town. \"Tomorrow,\" he said, \"we choose an outing that doesn't trigger a board meeting.\"","She took his hand. \"You said that before our wedding.\""],"questions":[],"wordCount":1070,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0128\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：港口诊所与失踪记录</div><h1>2. →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x011c03","title":"3. No Buildings Before Lunch","volume":"婚后外传：港口诊所与失踪记录","anchor":"chapter-0129","paragraphs":["By morning, Cassian had made a new holiday rule: no historical buildings before lunch. Mara agreed while packing sunscreen into her bag. Then Noor called to ask whether they could deliver a repaired drawer handle. \"Not a building,\" Mara pointed out. \"A very small piece of one.\"","He looked at her over his coffee. \"Your legal distinctions are becoming burdensome.\"","She kissed him until he forgot the objection, then stole his last piece of toast.","Their vacation home lay near a subway stop, so they left the hired car behind. She put the tickets in her wallet. A commuter showed them the quickest exit by the supermarket. The shop had donated fruit to the clinic; its manager wanted to know whether the staff had eaten any themselves. Mara promised to ask. Beyond the square, the retired submarine shone in clean morning light. The nearby tomb marker had acquired a wreath from an old dockworker. An export clerk passed the gateway, crossing the ditch on its little bridge. The port kept its dead in sight without making every living worker a monument.","At the caravanserai, the clinic's non-profit board had appointed an interim director. The former director had stepped aside while the undeclared investment was examined. The supervisor had been suspended by the transport firm, which was cooperating with investigators. Neither development guaranteed the full story. Mina's unpaid hours and the forged signature would be considered separately. Noor refused the suggestion that one apology should settle every consequence.","An official courier had delivered the replacement vaccine. The authorised team checked it against the required records before moving it into controlled storage. The thermostat had a clear service label, and a staff member now had paid time to review the maintenance log. The supplies with uncertain histories remained isolated. No dramatic recovery made them usable simply because readers wanted a happy ending.","Mara fixed the handle on the storehouse drawer. The repair was straightforward once the cabinet had been emptied properly. She cleaned the remaining metal filings into a tray for disposal after the investigator confirmed that the examination was finished. Mina practised closing it without force. \"I used to think every jam meant I had failed,\" she said.","\"Sometimes it means somebody put too much in the drawer,\" Mara replied.","Mina had considered quitting that morning. Not long ago, she would have obeyed the supervisor's threat rather than show anyone the message. Now she was deciding between returning under a new manager and taking paid training elsewhere. The aircraft mechanic had offered an introduction to an airline training adviser. He promised information, not an instant career. Both possibilities were better than being trapped by an accusation she could not challenge.","The clinic would renovate its cold room and improve the staff bedroom. Noor found a carpenter willing to upholster a worn chair using donated fabric. A pretty accessory would not counterbalance missing wages, but a sound chair was useful care. Mina felt mixed relief and anger. The staff arranged a cosy corner where a colleague could pause after a high stress condition. They would not make coping with exhaustion another unpaid obligation.","The cook had labelled a bread additive correctly instead of claiming the meal contained nothing unfamiliar. At the cafeteria, a buffet appeared on two borrowed tables. Cassian sliced bread while a little girl tied a balloon to the brim of his hat. He accepted this adornment as a serious professional duty. Mara leaned against his shoulder for a photo. \"The ducal standard has changed.\"","\"Strategically chosen for visibility.\"","A child brought him a drawing of a tractor. The actual vehicle was moving a barrel at the farm beyond the port, where staff were checking a brake before beginning work. The donated vegetables had followed an ordinary transport route. The next delivery would use the same marked transit arrangements, with no mysterious substitution. The clinic revised its transportation agreement accordingly and made the charges visible.","On the upper storey, the telegraph had stopped clicking. The guide opened its casing for an authorised repair while the aircraft mechanic examined the protected turbine. The little flywheel would turn again after a worn part was replaced. The promised flagship programme had only partially worked; paid training could put right some failures. A compact amplifier helped a visitor hear the explanation. The museum was not a spare-parts cupboard for the clinic; each budget would remain visible. That distinction was worth preserving even when everyone liked the same people.","Mina helped the volunteer sweep with a broom and clear the route to the toilet. The furnace inspector came through the cellar, then signed a maintenance report rather than offer reassurance from the doorway. A remnant tobacco label remained on the old wall. Its textual history explained how the building had been used before the clinic. A restrictive policy had once kept dock workers outside it; now their elected representatives would help review access.","Her testimony became a tipping point in the budget discussion. The board had to confront short-termism in its spending. A cheap contract could become expensive through risk, unpaid work and repeated repairs. That was true of more than vaccines. The director's replacement asked Mina to identify the hidden burdens in her route. She could traverse the docks with a map better than any manager could from a desk. Politically, inviting drivers to speak was less comfortable than praising them once a year. The board did it anyway.","An overprotective sister insisted on driving Mina home. Mina laughed and chose the bus, after arranging to meet her for dinner. The elastic tension in her shoulders had eased. Her unaffected pleasure made Mara smile. \"Care can leave someone room,\" she said to Cassian.","\"A difficult science. I'm studying.\"","The wind made the courtyard cloth whip once, then struck the gate violently. Cassian moved to grab it before it could slam on a boy's hand. Mara helped fasten it properly. An open gate was welcoming; an unsecured one was not. A slammed gate could trigger an injury. Nobody needed to turn that distinction into another speech.","At last they reached the zoo, still carrying the drawer's old handle by mistake. A keeper asked if it was a donation. Cassian said it was a warning about inviting restorers on holiday. Mara bought him a tiny porcelain cup from a stall selling locally made ceramic ware. It cost very little, and he held it as carefully as if it were priceless."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1074,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0129\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：港口诊所与失踪记录</div><h1>3.
